## L3 — Metformin: the epidemiology that launched it, the reversal, and TAME

### L3-01 **[承重]**

The founding claim. Bannister et al. 2014, UK CPRD primary-care records from 2000 to July 2013: 78,241 incident type 2 diabetics started on metformin monotherapy, 12,222 on sulphonylurea monotherapy, and 90,463 non-diabetic controls matched on age (±2y), sex, same GP practice, prior cancer status and smoking status. Total 503,384 person-years. Unadjusted all-cause mortality: 14.4 per 1,000 person-years for metformin vs 15.2 for its matched non-diabetic controls; 50.9 for sulphonylurea vs 28.7 for its controls. In a log-logistic accelerated failure time model, adjusted median survival time was 15% LOWER in the non-diabetic controls than in metformin initiators (survival time ratio 0.85, 95% CI 0.81-0.90). CRITICAL CALIBER: median follow-up only 2.4 years (mean 2.8); survival analysis truncated at 5.5 years; the survival clock started at index date + 180 days and both arms required 180 days' survival. This is a median-survival-time RATIO from a parametric extrapolation over <3 years of median observation, not an observed lifespan difference.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Bannister CA, Holden SE, Jenkins-Jones S, Morgan CLl, Halcox JP, Schernthaner G, Mukherjee J, Currie CJ. 'Can people with type 2 diabetes live longer than those without? A comparison of mortality in people initiated with metformin or sulphonylurea monotherapy and matched, non-diabetic controls.' Diabetes, Obesity and Metabolism, 2014. doi:10.1111/dom.12354
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2014-bannister.pdf
- 逐字引:> We identified 78 241 subjects treated with metformin, 12 222 treated with sulphonylurea, and 90 463 matched subjects without diabetes. This resulted in a total, censored follow-up period of 503 384 years. There were 7498 deaths in total, representing unadjusted mortality rates of 14.4 and 15.2, and 50.9 and 28.7 deaths per 1000 person-years for metformin monotherapy and their matched controls, and sulphonylurea monotherapy and their matched controls, respectively. With reference to observed survival in diabetic patients initiated with metformin monotherapy [survival time ratio (STR) = 1.0], adjusted median survival time was 15% lower (STR = 0.85, 95% CI 0.81-0.90) in matched individuals without diabetes
- 利益相关:Funded by AstraZeneca/Bristol-Myers Squibb. Author J. Mukherjee is an employee of Bristol-Myers Squibb. Senior author C.J. Currie is a director of Pharmatelligence, a consultancy funded by pharmaceutical companies; four other authors are employees or contractors of Pharmatelligence. Currie consults for AstraZeneca, BMS, Lilly, MSD, Sanofi-Aventis, Takeda and others.

### L3-02 **[承重]**

Bannister's own paper states it did NOT adjust for BMI, HbA1c, systolic blood pressure, cholesterol or creatinine, because those data were missing in the non-diabetic controls. This matters because the metformin group was markedly heavier: mean BMI 32.4 kg/m2 vs 27.4 in matched controls — a 5 kg/m2 gap left entirely out of the model. The metformin arm was also sicker on every measured axis (Charlson index 1.9 vs 0.7; prior major adverse cardiac event 10% vs 6%; GP contacts in prior year 11.3 vs 6.0; prior lipid-lowering therapy 50% vs 20%). The authors describe their own result as surprising given this.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Bannister CA et al., Diabetes Obes Metab 2014, Methods and Table 1
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2014-bannister.pdf
- 逐字引:> Glycated haemoglobin (HbA1c), systolic blood pressure, total cholesterol, creatinine and BMI were not considered because of large proportions of missing data in controls. […] Surprisingly, patients treated with metformin had a slightly longer adjusted survival than matched, non-diabetic controls, despite greater morbidity.
- 利益相关:See L3-01: AstraZeneca/Bristol-Myers Squibb funded.

### L3-03 **[承重]**

Informative censoring built into Bannister's design: metformin patients were censored 90 days after any regimen change, i.e. patients whose diabetes progressed enough to need add-on therapy were removed from the metformin arm. Non-diabetic controls had no analogous censoring rule. This systematically deletes deteriorating patients from the exposed arm while retaining them in the comparison arm. The 5.5-year truncation was justified on the grounds that average first-line monotherapy duration was 3 years — meaning the analytic window was set by drug-persistence, not by the biology of mortality.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Bannister CA et al., Diabetes Obes Metab 2014, Study Endpoint and Statistical Methods sections
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2014-bannister.pdf
- 逐字引:> the censor date, defined as the earliest of (i) the end of the recorded data, (ii) 90 days from regimen change or (iii) 5 years plus 180 days from the index date. […] The survival analysis was truncated at 5 1/2 years as the average duration of first-line monotherapy was 3 years.
- 利益相关:See L3-01.

### L3-04 

Bannister's authors themselves named the surveillance/treatment asymmetry that would produce their result artefactually — that diabetics receive lifestyle intervention and active blood-pressure and cholesterol monitoring while controls' asymptomatic risk factors go undiagnosed — and conceded that some controls likely had undiagnosed diabetes and that uncharacterised confounders may explain the group differences. These caveats are in the published paper; they did not travel with the headline.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Bannister CA et al., Diabetes Obes Metab 2014, Study Limitations section
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2014-bannister.pdf
- 逐字引:> As this was an observational study, patients were not randomized to treatment, and uncharacterized confounders may account for some of the differences between groups. […] it is probable that some controls had undiagnosed type 2 diabetes. […] people with type 2 diabetes are more likely to be receiving exercise and lifestyle interventions and close monitoring and control of blood pressure and cholesterol levels. Hypertension and hypercholesterolaemia are risk factors for cardiovascular disease but are generally asymptomatic. Therefore, these conditions may be less well diagnosed in the control group.
- 利益相关:See L3-01.

### L3-05 **[承重]**

THE REVERSAL. Keys et al. 2022, Danish national registers 1996-2012, three designs run in parallel. Matched cohort: 7,842 metformin monotherapy initiators vs 7,842 non-diabetics matched on birth year and sex. Metformin initiators had HIGHER mortality: crude 24.93 per 1,000 person-years (95% CI 23.23-26.64) vs 16.86 (15.46-18.27) in controls; HR 1.48 (1.32-1.64) unadjusted, 1.33 (1.16-1.54) fully adjusted. Nested case-control IRR 1.52 (1.37-1.68) unadjusted, 1.32 (1.16-1.50) adjusted. In 976 same-sex twin pairs discordant for diabetes — controlling for genetics, birth year and shared family environment — the effect was LARGER: HR 2.15 (1.48-3.13) unadjusted, 1.80 (1.11-2.91) adjusted; IRR 1.90 (1.35-2.67) unadjusted, 1.64 (1.10-2.46) adjusted. Increased mortality was seen at every level of cumulative exposure (1-200, 201-500, 501-1000, 1000+ defined daily doses) — no protective dose-response.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Keys MT, Thinggaard M, Larsen LA, Pedersen DA, Hallas J, Christensen K. 'Reassessing the evidence of a survival advantage in Type 2 diabetes treated with metformin compared with controls without diabetes: a retrospective cohort study.' International Journal of Epidemiology 2022;51(6):1886-1898. PMID 36287641
- URL:https://doi.org/10.1093/ije/dyac200
- 逐字引:> In case-control pairs matched on birth year and sex or co-twin (sex, birth year and familial factors), incident Type 2 diabetes with treatment by metformin monotherapy initiation compared with no diagnosis of diabetes was associated with increased mortality in both singletons (IRR = 1.52, 95% CI: 1.37, 1.68) and discordant twin pairs (IRR = 1.90, 95% CI: 1.35, 2.67). […] Treatment initiation by metformin monotherapy in Type 2 diabetes was not associated with survival equal or superior to that of the general population without diabetes.
- 利益相关:Funded by the Velux Foundation (grant 31205) to the Danish Ageing Research Center. 'Conflict of interest: None declared.' No industry funding — the opposite pattern to Bannister.

### L3-06 **[承重]**

THE FORENSIC SMOKING GUN. Keys et al. placed their own crude mortality rates beside Bannister's. Three of the four cells replicate almost exactly across the two countries — non-diabetic controls matched to metformin 16.9 (Denmark) vs 15.2 (Bannister); non-diabetic controls matched to sulfonylurea 28.4 vs 28.7; sulfonylurea initiators 49.0 vs 50.9. Only ONE cell diverges, and it is the metformin arm: 24.9 in Denmark vs 14.4 in Bannister. The anomaly is confined to the exposed group in the original study, which is the signature of a selection or censoring artefact in that arm rather than a population difference between the UK and Denmark. Keys et al. further report that sensitivity analyses reproducing Bannister's suspected biases had only marginal impact and did not explain the contrast.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Keys MT et al., Int J Epidemiol 2022;51(6):1886-1898, Discussion
- URL:https://doi.org/10.1093/ije/dyac200
- 逐字引:> without diabetes were almost identical (metformin matched cohort without diabetes, 16.9 vs 15.2; sulfonylurea matched cohort without diabetes, 28.4 vs 28.7), as well as for sulfonylurea monotherapy initiators (49.0 vs 50.9). Differences in crude mortality rates were only observed in those initiating metformin monotherapy (24.9 in our study vs 14.4 in Bannister et al.). Lastly, various sensitivity analyses suggested that the potential biases present in Bannister et al. (2014), specifically due to informative censoring of metformin initiators and a disadvantageous selection bias into the control group without diabetes, likely had only marginal impact.
- 利益相关:Velux Foundation; none declared.

### L3-07 **[承重]**

THE TRUNCATION EXPLAINED. Stevenson-Hoare et al. 2023 repeated the Bannister comparison in Wales (SAIL database) with a much larger cohort — 129,140 metformin-treated and 68,563 sulphonylurea-treated type 2 diabetics with non-diabetic controls matched on sex, age, smoking, and history of cancer and cardiovascular disease — and analysed a range of study-window lengths up to 20 years. Metformin showed a survival benefit over matched controls within the first three years, but this REVERSED after five years; over the full 20 years metformin-treated diabetics had SHORTER survival than matched non-diabetic controls. Bannister truncated at 5.5 years with a 2.4-year median follow-up, i.e. the original analytic window sat precisely on the segment of the curve where the transient benefit lives. Same country, same kind of primary-care record system, opposite conclusion once the window is long enough to be about longevity.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Stevenson-Hoare J, Leonenko G, Escott-Price V. 'Comparison of long-term effects of metformin on longevity between people with type 2 diabetes and matched non-diabetic controls.' BMC Public Health 2023 May 2;23(1):804. doi:10.1186/s12889-023-15764-y. PMID 37131166
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37131166/
- 逐字引:> Using the full twenty-year period, we found that type 2 diabetes patients treated with metformin had shorter survival time than matched controls, as did sulphonylurea patients. Metformin patients had better survival than sulphonylurea patients, controlling for age. Within the first three years, metformin therapy showed a benefit over matched controls, but this reversed after five years of treatment. […] While metformin does appear to confer benefits to longevity in the short term, these initial benefits are outweighed by the effects of type 2 diabetes when patients are observed over a period of up to twenty years.
- 利益相关:None found. I could not reach the paywalled full text, so hazard ratios at the 3/5/20-year timepoints are not available — the direction and reversal point are from the abstract.

### L3-08 **[承重]**

HOW MUCH THE BIAS IS WORTH, MEASURED. Khouri, Dell'Aniello, Yin, Azoulay and Suissa 2025 used the same database as Bannister (UK CPRD) to quantify immortal time bias directly. Among 13,314 women with newly diagnosed breast cancer and type 2 diabetes (1998-2020), a prevalent new-user design matched 4,923 metformin initiators 1:1 to 4,923 nonusers on prior diabetes diagnosis and time-conditional propensity score. Properly designed: all-cause mortality HR 0.96 (95% CI 0.89-1.05) and breast-cancer mortality HR 1.12 (0.98-1.28) — null. When the same data were analysed with the flawed approach that ignores immortal time bias, the large beneficial effects reported in the older literature reappeared. The metformin survival signal is reproducibly an artefact of design, demonstrable within a single dataset.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Khouri C, Dell'Aniello S, Yin H, Azoulay L, Suissa S. 'Metformin to treat breast cancer: an application of the prevalent new-user design for observational studies with no active comparator.' Journal of Clinical Epidemiology 2025. PMID 40578405
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40578405/
- 逐字引:> the HRs of breast cancer-related and all-cause mortality were 1.12 (95% CI: 0.98-1.28) and 0.96 (95% CI: 0.89-1.05), respectively.
- 利益相关:Suissa reports consulting/advisory relationships and speaking fees from multiple pharmaceutical companies (per his disclosure in Keys et al. 2025). No metformin manufacturer interest identified.

### L3-09 

The original bias catalogue. Suissa and Azoulay 2012 reviewed 24 influential observational studies of metformin and cancer and found 13 affected by immortal time bias, 9 by time-window bias, and 3 by time-lag bias. The mechanism: the interval between cohort entry and the first metformin prescription is 'immortal' — the patient must survive to receive it — and is misclassified as exposed time, manufacturing an apparent survival benefit. These are the same time-related biases that operate on the metformin-mortality literature generally.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Suissa S, Azoulay L. 'Metformin and the risk of cancer: time-related biases in observational studies.' Diabetes Care 2012;35(12):2665-2673. PMID 23173135
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23173135/
- 利益相关:Suissa has consulting/advisory relationships with multiple pharmaceutical companies. I could not reach the full text to quote verbatim; the 13/9/3 breakdown is reported consistently in secondary descriptions of this paper and restated in Suissa's 2023 Diabetes Care follow-up 'Metformin and Cancer: Solutions to a Real-World Evidence Failure'.

### L3-10 **[承重]**

THE HEALTHY-ADHERER EFFECT IS LARGER THAN THE METFORMIN EFFECT. Simpson et al.'s BMJ 2006 meta-analysis of 21 studies (46,847 participants), including 8 placebo arms (19,633 participants), found that good versus poor adherence was associated with an all-cause mortality odds ratio of 0.56 (95% CI 0.50-0.63) — and that good adherence to PLACEBO carried an essentially identical benefit, OR 0.56 (0.43-0.74), statistically indistinguishable from good adherence to active drug (0.55, 0.49-0.62). Bannister required patients to have taken metformin for at least 180 days to enter the cohort, i.e. selected on adherence; the non-diabetic controls were selected on adherence to nothing. A 15% median-survival-time advantage is comfortably inside the magnitude of the pure adherence artefact.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Simpson SH, Eurich DT, Majumdar SR, Padwal RS, Tsuyuki RT, Varney J, Johnson JA. 'A meta-analysis of the association between adherence to drug therapy and mortality.' BMJ 2006;333(7557):15. PMID 16790458
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16790458/
- 利益相关:None identified. I reached this via search-result reporting of the abstract rather than the BMJ full text, so the numbers (OR 0.56 [0.50-0.63] overall; placebo 0.56 [0.43-0.74]; active 0.55 [0.49-0.62]; 21 studies/46,847 participants; 8 placebo arms/19,633) should be re-verified against the BMJ page before publication.

### L3-11 **[承重]**

TAME's ACTUAL STATUS, VERIFIED: it has never enrolled a patient and is not registered. As of 2026-07-28 I queried the ClinicalTrials.gov API v2 three separate ways — a title query for 'Targeting Aging with Metformin', a term query for 'metformin aging TAME', and an OfficialTitle/BriefTitle TAME query restricted to intervention=metformin. None returned any ~3,000-participant Wake Forest / AFAR trial; the last returned zero studies. Registration on ClinicalTrials.gov is a legal precondition for enrolling a participant in a US applicable clinical trial, so the absence of an NCT number establishes zero enrolment. Separately, AFAR's own TAME page fetched the same day is written entirely in future tense and fundraising language — it asks for 'Visionary donors to help launch' the trial and says the study 'will engage over 3,000 individuals'. No launch date, no enrolment figure, no NCT number. Design as described: ~3,000 adults aged 65-79, 14 sites, ~6 years, coordinated by Wake Forest University School of Medicine.

- 来源类型:official-primary
- 出处:ClinicalTrials.gov API v2, three queries executed 2026-07-28, all returning no TAME study; and American Federation for Aging Research, TAME Trial page, fetched 2026-07-28
- URL:https://clinicaltrials.gov/api/v2/studies?query.titles=Targeting%20Aging%20with%20Metformin
- 逐字引:> AFAR is leading fundraising efforts to support and launch the TAME Trial.
- 利益相关:AFAR is the body fundraising for TAME, so its framing is interested. Nir Barzilai, TAME's architect, sits on AFAR's board, is a founder of CohBar Inc. and a medical advisor to Life Biosciences.

### L3-12 **[承重]**

HOW LONG TAME HAS BEEN ABOUT TO START — the architect's own words. Speaking to STAT News in August 2022, Nir Barzilai said he had been publicly predicting TAME's imminent start every year since roughly 2015, and that he had lost credibility as a result. He also described the trial's purpose in explicitly regulatory rather than scientific terms: the point is to obtain an FDA indication for aging, not to discover anything new about metformin. Counting from the 2015 FDA meeting that produced the composite endpoint, TAME has been imminent for approximately eleven years as of 2026. STAT reported the required budget as $30-50 million, with NIH having provided roughly $9 million for biomarker work only.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:STAT News, 'As billionaires race to fund anti-aging projects, a much-discussed trial goes overlooked', 9 August 2022, quoting Nir Barzilai
- URL:https://www.statnews.com/2022/08/09/anti-aging-projects-funding-much-discussed-trial-overlooked/
- 逐字引:> I'm on the record saying I'm pretty sure it will start this year, for the last seven years. I have no credibility anymore. It's just incredibly frustrating. But it's going to happen, because it has to happen. […] There's nothing in that clinical trial that hasn't already been shown before with metformin. We are just trying to package it all up and call it aging so the FDA will approve this for aging.
- 利益相关:Barzilai: founder of CohBar Inc., medical advisor to Life Biosciences, AFAR board member, president of the Academy for Health & Lifespan Research. Budget figures come from journalism, not from a primary AFAR grant or audited document — no such primary document was locatable.

### L3-13 **[承重]**

BARZILAI'S MOST RECENT PUBLIC STATEMENT ON TAME, dated 12 August 2025: he cannot give an update because the trial 'is now being handled within ARPA-H', and expects the TAME design to be used by Eli Lilly for a GLP-1 agonist instead. He describes everything as on hold. He also confirms the composite endpoint design and that TAME cannot produce a standalone mortality number. He does not claim the trial has started; the interviewer describes it as 'long-awaited'. Barzilai's framing has shifted from TAME-as-trial to TAME-as-template: its value, he says, is as a regulatory template for the pharmaceutical industry, which is why Lilly is interested.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:Arkadi Mazin, 'Nir Barzilai: "Positive Evidence for Metformin is Mounting"', Lifespan.io, 12 August 2025 (interview conducted in response to the Keys et al. 2025 review)
- URL:https://lifespan.io/news/nir-barzilai-positive-evidence-for-metformin-is-mounting/
- 逐字引:> I cannot give you a perfect update because it's now being handled within ARPA-H. I think there will be two major trials that come out of this. One is going to be from Eli Lilly; they're going to do a TAME-like study but with their GLP-1 agonist. […] This conversation is ongoing, and we're holding everything because I would love for all four major drug classes to be tested so we can get comparisons. […] TAME is designed to measure a cluster of outcomes; it doesn't look at mortality independently. The primary endpoint is a cluster of cardiovascular disease, cognitive decline, cancer, and then mortality. So, you're not going to get a single mortality number out of it.
- 利益相关:Barzilai: founder of CohBar Inc., medical advisor to Life Biosciences, AFAR board member. Lifespan.io is a longevity-advocacy publication, not a neutral outlet.

### L3-14 **[承重]**

THE DECISIVE HUMAN CLINICAL-OUTCOME EVIDENCE, AND IT IS NULL. The Diabetes Prevention Program randomised 3,234 adults with impaired glucose tolerance (i.e. NON-diabetics) in 1996-99 to placebo (n=1,073), metformin 850mg twice daily (n=1,082), or intensive lifestyle (n=1,079), and followed them a median of 21 years. Primary outcome — first nonfatal myocardial infarction, stroke, or cardiovascular death, adjudicated: metformin vs placebo HR 1.03 (95% CI 0.78-1.37, p=0.81), 101 events vs 98. Extended cardiovascular outcome: HR 1.00 (0.80-1.25, p=0.99). This is the longest randomised follow-up of metformin in people without diabetes that exists, and the point estimate is exactly 1.0.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Goldberg RB, et al. 'Effects of Long-term Metformin and Lifestyle Interventions on Cardiovascular Events in the Diabetes Prevention Program and its Outcome Study.' Circulation 2022;145(22):1632-1641. PMC9179081
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9179081/
- 逐字引:> Neither metformin nor lifestyle reduced major cardiovascular events in DPPOS over 21 years despite long-term prevention of diabetes.
- 利益相关:Funded by NIDDK/NIH and CDC; Merck KGaA provided study medication. One author (Gadde) reports grant support from AstraZeneca and BioKer. No metformin-seller involvement in analysis.

### L3-15 **[承重]**

The same DPP/DPPOS cohort, mortality endpoints, median 21 years, 453 deaths (cancer 170, cardiovascular 131). Metformin versus placebo: all-cause mortality HR 0.99 (95% CI 0.79-1.25); cancer mortality HR 1.04 (0.72-1.52); cardiovascular mortality HR 1.08 (0.70-1.66). Lifestyle versus placebo was equally null. The dissociation is the crux of the whole line: over the same 21 years these interventions DID prevent diabetes (lifestyle -24%, 95% CI 15-32; metformin -17%) and still moved no mortality endpoint at all. The drug does what it is licensed to do and nothing downstream.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Diabetes Prevention Program Research Group. 'Effect of Metformin and Lifestyle Interventions on Mortality in the Diabetes Prevention Program and Diabetes Prevention Program Outcomes Study.' Diabetes Care 2021;44(12):2775-2782. PMID 34697033
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34697033/
- 逐字引:> Compared with placebo, metformin did not influence mortality from all causes (HR 0.99 [95% CI 0.79, 1.25]), cancer (HR 1.04 [95% CI 0.72, 1.52]), or cardiovascular disease (HR 1.08 [95% CI 0.70, 1.66]).
- 利益相关:NIDDK/NIH funded. NOTE: the ADA journal site returned 403; I obtained these hazard ratios from search-result reporting of the published abstract, not from the full text. Re-verify before publication.

### L3-16 **[承重]**

THE ONLY ONGOING HARD-OUTCOME RCT, AND IT DOES NOT REPORT UNTIL 2029. VA-IMPACT (NCT02915198), Investigation of Metformin in Pre-Diabetes on Atherosclerotic Cardiovascular Outcomes. Verified directly against the ClinicalTrials.gov API on 2026-07-28: status RECRUITING; lead sponsor VA Office of Research and Development; start date 3 April 2023; primary completion AND completion date both 28 September 2029; estimated enrolment 7,410; last update posted 17 December 2025. Population is prediabetes with established atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease; metformin extended-release titrated to 2,000 mg/day versus placebo. Any assertion today that metformin reduces cardiovascular events in prediabetes is being made three years ahead of the trial designed to test it.

- 来源类型:official-primary
- 出处:ClinicalTrials.gov, NCT02915198, VA-IMPACT, record queried via API v2 on 2026-07-28
- URL:https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT02915198
- 逐字引:> Time in days to death, non-fatal myocardial infarction, stroke, hospitalization for unstable angina, or symptom-driven coronary revascularization
- 利益相关:US Department of Veterans Affairs funded; no commercial sponsor.

### L3-17 **[承重]**

THE NEWEST RCT — 2026, in non-diabetics, with an aging endpoint — IS NULL. METFORAGING, published 2026: double-blind, randomised, placebo-controlled pilot at La Paz University Hospital, Madrid. Non-diabetic, virally suppressed people with HIV aged 50+, CD4 >500, randomised 1:1 to 850 mg metformin twice daily or placebo for 96 weeks. Randomised n=19 metformin / n=21 placebo (40 total); per-protocol n=17 / n=18. Primary outcome was the adjusted between-group difference in epigenetic age acceleration by the PhenoAge clock at week 96. Result: -1.02 years (95% CI -5.30 to 3.26; p=0.627). Caliber caveat that must travel with this: n=40 is underpowered, the confidence interval spans -5.3 to +3.3 years and cannot exclude a real benefit. But it is the first randomised test of metformin against a clock endpoint in non-diabetics at nearly two years of exposure, and it does not even move the surrogate.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Marcelo-Calvo C, Esteban-Cantos A, Jurado F, et al. 'Metformin and epigenetic age in non-diabetic older people with HIV in Madrid (METFORAGING): a double-blind, randomised, placebo-controlled, pilot trial.' EClinicalMedicine 2026;95:103874. PMID 42023167
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42023167/
- 逐字引:> At week 96, the adjusted between-group difference (metformin vs. placebo) for PhenoAge EAA was -1.02 years (95% confidence interval [CI] -5.30 to 3.26; p = 0.627). […] Although no significant difference was noted in the primary outcome between groups, these preliminary findings support the feasibility of geroscience-trials in this population and support further investigation of metformin in larger, adequately powered studies.
- 利益相关:Not captured from the abstract; funding and COI not retrieved.

### L3-18 **[承重]**

WHAT THE 'MILES TRIAL SHOWS METFORMIN SLOWS HUMAN AGING' CLAIM ACTUALLY RESTS ON: fourteen people, six weeks, messenger RNA. MILES was a randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled CROSSOVER trial in n=14 participants of approximately 70 years, treated 6 weeks each with metformin and placebo. Endpoints were gene expression in skeletal muscle and subcutaneous adipose biopsies plus a mixed-meal challenge: 647 genes differentially expressed in muscle and 146 in adipose tissue at FDR<0.05. No clinical outcome and no aging outcome was measured. The authors' claim is 'first evidence that, in older adults, metformin has metabolic and nonmetabolic effects linked to aging' — a transcriptomic pilot, routinely promoted in popular longevity media as human evidence that metformin slows aging.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Kulkarni AS, Brutsaert EF, Anghel V, et al. 'Metformin regulates metabolic and nonmetabolic pathways in skeletal muscle and subcutaneous adipose tissues of older adults.' Aging Cell 2018;17(2):e12723. PMID 29383869
- URL:https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29383869/
- 逐字引:> We studied ~70-year-old participants (n = 14) in a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, crossover trial in which they were treated with 6 weeks each of metformin and placebo. […] Using FDR<0.05, 647 genes were differentially expressed in muscle and 146 genes were differentially expressed in adipose tissue.
- 利益相关:Not captured. The MILES investigators overlap with the TAME/Barzilai group at Albert Einstein College of Medicine.

### L3-19 **[承重]**

COUNTER-EVIDENCE 1: metformin blunts the mitochondrial and insulin-sensitivity adaptations to aerobic exercise. Konopka et al. 2019: 53 older adults (mean age 62 ± 1) randomised to metformin (n=27, titrated to 2,000 mg/day; 11 reduced to 1,500 mg/day for GI intolerance) or placebo (n=26), all completing 12 weeks of aerobic exercise training. Metformin abolished the ~25% training-induced increase in maximal complex-I-linked mitochondrial respiration and attenuated the increase in peripheral insulin sensitivity by 30-50%. CALIBER WARNING widely misreported: the VO2max effect — a ~50% attenuation of the training-induced increase — did NOT reach statistical significance (p=0.08) and must not be cited as significant.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Konopka AR, Laurin JL, Schoenberg HM, et al. 'Metformin inhibits mitochondrial adaptations to aerobic exercise training in older adults.' Aging Cell 2019;18(1):e12880. PMC6351883
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6351883
- 逐字引:> Our findings suggest that combining two healthspan extending treatments, metformin and exercise, may interfere with the improvement in some parameters of physiological function and do not interact synergistically.
- 利益相关:Funded by Dairy Management, Inc. (to BFM & KLH) and Dexcom, Inc. (to ARK) — commercial funders with no metformin interest, but unusual funders for an exercise-physiology trial. No conflicts of interest reported by the authors.

### L3-20 **[承重]**

COUNTER-EVIDENCE 2, the strongest: the MASTERS trial, in which the investigators' prespecified hypothesis was the OPPOSITE of what they found. Randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicentre: 109 randomised (55 placebo, 54 metformin), 94 completed (48 placebo, 46 metformin), aged 64.4-91.2 years (median 69.3), metformin 1,700 mg/day, 14 weeks of supervised progressive resistance training. Lean body mass: placebo +1.95% (SD 2.69, p<.001) vs metformin +0.41% (SD 2.25, p=.218), between-group p=.003. Thigh muscle mass: placebo +3.90% (SD 5.54, p<.001) vs metformin +0.45% (SD 3.95, p=.441), between-group p<.001. In the metformin arm neither gain reached significance. Report alongside: type II fibre cross-sectional area did NOT differ between groups (placebo +18.5% vs metformin +14.5%, p=.610), so the whole-muscle effect is not cleanly explained at the fibre level. The authors had hypothesised metformin would AUGMENT the response via reduced inflammation.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Walton RG, Dungan CM, Long DE, et al. 'Metformin blunts muscle hypertrophy in response to progressive resistance exercise training in older adults: A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter trial: The MASTERS trial.' Aging Cell 2019;18:e13039. PMC6826125
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6826125/
- 逐字引:> These results underscore the benefits of PRT in older adults, but metformin negatively impacts the hypertrophic response to resistance training in healthy older individuals.
- 利益相关:Publicly funded: NIA R01AG046920, NCATS UL1TR001998 and UL1TR003096. 'The authors have no conflict of interests to disclose.' NOTE: a corrigendum exists (Aging Cell, PMC7059173) correcting the relative-strength row of Table 2 — corrected values placebo +18.9% (19.1) vs metformin +14.8% (18.3), p=0.30. The lean-mass and thigh-mass primary results were unaffected; use the corrected strength figures.

### L3-21 **[承重]**

THE ADVOCATE CONCEDES THE EXERCISE POINT. Barzilai, in the same August 2025 interview in which he defends metformin against the Keys review, states that people under 50 without diabetes should not take metformin, explicitly because of its interference with muscle building and VO2max. This is a rare point of agreement between the field's leading proponent and its critics, and it is consequential: it means the drug's strongest advocate accepts that metformin degrades adaptation to the intervention with the best-established human outcome evidence in all of geroscience.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:Arkadi Mazin, 'Nir Barzilai: "Positive Evidence for Metformin is Mounting"', Lifespan.io, 12 August 2025
- URL:https://lifespan.io/news/nir-barzilai-positive-evidence-for-metformin-is-mounting/
- 逐字引:> Whoever takes metformin who doesn't have diabetes and is not at least 50 years old is making a mistake, in particular if they're trying to build their muscle or increase their VO2max.
- 利益相关:Barzilai: CohBar founder, Life Biosciences advisor, AFAR board member.

### L3-22 **[承重]**

THE MOUSE BOTTOM LINE (stated for contrast only; L1 owns the ITP in depth). The NIA Interventions Testing Program tested metformin at 1,000 ppm (0.1%) in the diet from 9 months of age in genetically heterogeneous UM-HET3 mice across three sites: 148 male and 140 female metformin-treated mice versus 294 male and 281 female controls. Metformin alone produced a 7% increase in median lifespan in males that was NOT statistically significant (P=0.35), no effect in females, and no maximum-lifespan effect in either sex. Keys et al. 2025 restate it as no benefit in either sex or in any secondary analysis. The contrast that structures the article: rapamycin, tested in the same programme with the same mice and the same log-rank statistics, extends lifespan robustly and repeatedly. Whatever the ITP may be insensitive to, it was not insensitive to rapamycin.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Strong R, Miller RA, Antebi A, et al. (NIA Interventions Testing Program). 'Longer lifespan in male mice treated with a weakly estrogenic agonist, an antioxidant, an alpha-glucosidase inhibitor or a Nrf2-inducer.' Aging Cell 2016;15(5):872-884. PMC5013015; and Keys MT, Hallas J, Miller RA, Suissa S, Christensen K, Ageing Res Rev 2025;111:102817
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5013015/
- 逐字引:> led to a 7% increase in median lifespan of males when data were pooled across sites, but the effect was not statistically significant (P = 0.35)
- 利益相关:NIA-funded programme; Richard Miller is an ITP principal investigator and also a co-author of the 2025 sceptical review, which Barzilai characterises as motivated defence of the ITP.

### L3-23 **[承重]**

THE HYPED PRIMATE STUDY, DECOMPOSED. Yang et al., Cell 2024, is a 'proof-of-concept' Resource article in MALE cynomolgus monkeys aged 13-16 years at start (16-19 at endpoint), given 20 mg/kg/day metformin in drinking water for 1,200 days (~3.3 years) within a 40-month study. The group sizes, from the figure legends: metformin-treated (O-Met) n=6; old control (O-Ctrl) n=5; plus young control n=6 (aged 3-5) and middle-aged control n=3 (aged 10-12). ONE old control monkey died of kidney failure on day 1,126. THE TREATED GROUP IS SIX MONKEYS. The famous '6 years' is not a brain measurement and not a clinical outcome: it is 6.41 years on a plasma PROTEOMIC clock, with frontal-lobe DNA-methylation age restored by 6.10 years; per-tissue transcriptAge deltas run 3.40-5.31 years. All of these clocks were built by the same group from 36 monkeys in the same colony.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Yang Y, Lu X, Liu N, Ma S, Zhang H, Zhang Z, et al.; Qu J, Zhang W, Liu G-H (corresponding). 'Metformin decelerates aging clock in male monkeys.' Cell 2024;187:6358-6378.e29, 31 October 2024. doi:10.1016/j.cell.2024.08.021
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2024-yang.pdf
- 逐字引:> This analysis estimated that metformin administration resets the proteinAge of treated monkeys by an average of 6.41 years […] DNAmAge was also rescued, including the frontal lobe, lung, kidney (cortex), liver, and skin, which were restored by an average of 6.10, 5.11, 4.90, 3.95, and 2.65 years, respectively […] Y-Ctrl, n = 6; M-Ctrl, n = 3; O-Ctrl, n = 5; O-Met, n = 6 monkeys.
- 利益相关:Declaration of interests, verbatim: 'J.C.I.B., S.H., C.R.E., P.R., and A.H. are employees of Altos Labs. S.H. is a founder of the non-profit Epigenetic Clock Development Foundation, which has licensed several of his patents from UC Regents and distributes the mammalian methylation array.' The headline endpoint of this paper is an epigenetic clock number and a co-author holds licensed patents on epigenetic clocks and distributes the array used to generate them — an interest in the ENDPOINT, not merely the compound. Otherwise funded by Chinese public science agencies (NSFC, MOST, CAS); no metformin seller involved.

### L3-24 **[承重]**

THE MONKEY STUDY'S AUTHORS SAY, IN THEIR OWN LIMITATIONS SECTION, THAT THEY DID NOT MEASURE SURVIVAL AND THAT FUNCTIONAL EVIDENCE IS SPARSE. A study that never assessed mortality was reported worldwide as evidence that a drug slows aging. This single sentence is the most useful thing in the paper for calibrating the gap between what was measured and what was claimed on its behalf.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Yang Y et al., Cell 2024;187:6358-6378.e29, 'Limitations of the study'
- URL:https://gwern.net/doc/longevity/metformin/2024-yang.pdf
- 逐字引:> We have noted that metformin potentially slows aging at molecular and tissue levels in males, but conclusive evidence of functional rejuvenation is sparse, and a full understanding of its side effects in humans remains elusive. The study also omitted assessments of monkey mortality and the long-term effects following metformin cessation.
- 利益相关:See L3-23 (Altos Labs employment; epigenetic-clock patent interest).

### L3-25 **[承重]**

THE ONE POSITIVE 2025 TARGET TRIAL EMULATION — AND WHAT IT ACTUALLY COMPARES. Shadyab et al. 2025, Women's Health Initiative, new-user active-comparator design with 1:1 propensity-score matching. N = 438 TOTAL (219 metformin, 219 sulfonylurea), postmenopausal women aged 60+, mean age 70.0 at initiation, 84.9% White. Outcome: death before age 90. Incidence 3.7 vs 5.0 per 100 person-years; adjusted HR 0.70 (95% CI 0.56-0.88); E-value 1.88. CALIBER, and this is the whole point: the comparator is SULFONYLUREA, not non-diabetics and not placebo. This study is fully compatible with 'sulfonylureas are harmful' and says nothing about metformin versus no drug. It cannot support the Bannister claim; it does not test it. An E-value of 1.88 means an unmeasured confounder of modest strength would null it — and BMI and renal function, which govern whether a patient gets metformin or a sulfonylurea, differ by roughly that much.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Shadyab AH, Espeland MA, Odegaard AO, Manson JE, Johnson KC, Chen Z, Zhu B, Howard B, LaCroix AZ. 'Comparative Effectiveness of Metformin Versus Sulfonylureas on Exceptional Longevity in Women With Type 2 Diabetes: Target Trial Emulation.' The Journals of Gerontology: Series A 2025;80(7):glaf095
- URL:https://academic.oup.com/biomedgerontology/article/80/7/glaf095/8137954
- 逐字引:> In this first target trial emulation of metformin and exceptional longevity, we found that metformin initiation increased exceptional longevity compared with sulfonylurea initiation among women with type 2 diabetes. […] Because this comparison was not made to placebo in a randomized controlled trial and given the observational design with potential for residual confounding, causality cannot be inferred.
- 利益相关:Funded by NHLBI/NIH (75N92021D00001-75N92021D00005). Most authors declare none; B. Howard received fees from pharmaceutical companies for unrelated lectures.

### L3-26 **[承重]**

THE FIELD'S OWN SYNTHESIS HAS TURNED. A 2025 review in Ageing Research Reviews authored jointly by the Danish replication team (Keys, Hallas, Christensen), the ITP's Richard Miller, and Samy Suissa — i.e. the mouse-lifespan and the pharmacoepidemiology critics in one paper — concludes that the evidence base is moving toward uncertainty, not confirmation. It names the two specific defects in Bannister: informative censoring of metformin initiators when they intensified treatment, and 'never-diabetic' control selection conditioned on the full future follow-up, which artificially raises control-group mortality. It catalogues the null clinical trials in non-diabetics: MA.32 (adjuvant metformin in breast cancer, no improvement in overall survival, HR 1.10), CAMERA, MetCAB and GIPS-III (no effect on primary cardiovascular outcomes), COVID-OUT (no effect on primary outcome), TOGETHER (no effect on any primary or secondary outcome), MET-PREVENT (poorly tolerated, no physical improvement), and DPPOS.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Keys MT, Hallas J, Miller RA, Suissa S, Christensen K. 'Emerging uncertainty on the anti-aging potential of metformin.' Ageing Research Reviews 2025 Sep;111:102817. doi:10.1016/j.arr.2025.102817. PMID 40582648; PMCID PMC13329735
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13329735/
- 逐字引:> matched control groups were selected as being 'never-diabetic' by conditioning across the full range of future follow-up. This can potentially artificially increase mortality in the control group.
- 利益相关:Velux Foundation grant to Kaare Christensen; funders had no role. Samy Suissa reports consulting/advisory relationships and speaking fees from multiple pharmaceutical companies; Jesper Hallas reports pharma grant funding; other authors declare none. Note Barzilai's rebuttal that Richard Miller's co-authorship makes this a defence of the ITP's null result — worth reporting as a contested framing.

### L3-27 

HYPE ARTEFACT, RECORDED AS SUCH, NOT AS EVIDENCE. A page at imedic.health dated 2 March 2026 and bylined only to an 'iMedic Medical Editorial Team' reports TAME trial RESULTS as fact — a significant reduction in the composite incidence of cardiovascular disease, cancer, dementia and mortality versus placebo, plus improvements in DNA methylation clocks — complete with plausible design details (about 3,000 non-diabetic adults, ages 65-79, 14 US centres, 1,500 mg/day). No primary source is cited for any result; the reference list contains only the 2016 Cell Metabolism design paper and the 2014 Bannister observational study. TAME has never enrolled a participant and has no ClinicalTrials.gov registration. This is a fabricated readout for a trial that does not exist, ranking in search results. More broadly, several AI-generated sites (welzo, stackingyears, mental-momentum, progevita, protosi) publish mutually contradictory 2026 TAME status claims with no sourcing, and the fabrications run consistently in the positive direction.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:iMedic Medical Editorial Team, 'Metformin Anti-Aging: TAME Trial Results 2026', imedic.health, 2 March 2026 — recorded as an example of fabricated evidence
- URL:https://imedic.health/en/health/news/metformin-anti-aging-tame-trial-results-2026
- 逐字引:> Participants receiving metformin 1500mg daily showed a significant reduction in the composite incidence of cardiovascular disease, cancer, dementia, and mortality compared to placebo.
- 利益相关:Unattributed AI-generated content site; no named author, no disclosed funding, no primary citations for the claimed results.

**交叉口径问题:**
- THE CENTRAL CALIBER ERROR: 'diabetics on metformin outlive non-diabetics' is a 15% median-survival-TIME RATIO from a log-logistic accelerated-failure-time extrapolation, over a 2.4-year MEDIAN follow-up truncated at 5.5 years, with BMI (32.4 vs 27.4 kg/m2) unadjusted and progressing patients censored out of the exposed arm. The crude rates it rests on differ by 0.8 deaths per 1,000 person-years (14.4 vs 15.2). It is universally repeated as an observed LIFESPAN difference. A claim about longevity was built on under three years of median observation.
- THE DENMARK/UK DISAGREEMENT IS ONE CELL, NOT FOUR. Three of Bannister's four crude mortality rates replicate almost exactly in Denmark (controls-vs-metformin 15.2/16.9; controls-vs-sulfonylurea 28.7/28.4; sulfonylurea 50.9/49.0). Only the metformin arm diverges (14.4 vs 24.9). Barzilai attributes the disagreement to population differences (UK obesity 20% vs Danish 5%). That explanation cannot be right: a population difference would move all four cells, not one. Anyone using the 'different populations' defence must account for the three matching cells.
- THE 2025 TARGET TRIAL EMULATION IS BEING QUOTED AS IF IT RESTORED THE BANNISTER CLAIM. It does not. Its comparator is SULFONYLUREA (n=219 vs 219, women only, mean age 70), not non-diabetics and not placebo. HR 0.70 (0.56-0.88) for death before 90 versus a drug the same literature says is harmful is not evidence about metformin versus no drug. E-value 1.88 means a modest unmeasured confounder nulls it — and BMI/renal function, the very variables that determine which drug a patient receives, differ by about that much.
- BARZILAI MISQUOTES THE MONKEY STUDY IN THE DIRECTION OF HIS OWN THESIS. On 12 August 2025 he described it as 'Aging was delayed by eight years on the transcriptomic level.' The paper's transcriptAge deltas top out at 5.31 years (Achilles tendon), with liver 4.14, bronchus 3.71, muscle 3.56, lung 3.40. The ~6.41-year figure is PROTEOMIC and the ~6.10-year figure is frontal-lobe METHYLATION. There is no 8-year transcriptomic number anywhere in the paper.
- BARZILAI ALSO PRE-ANNOUNCED THE J GERONTOL PAPER AS SHOWING 'people on metformin are twice as likely to reach age 90 as other people with diabetes.' The published estimate is HR 0.70 (0.56-0.88) for death before 90 versus sulfonylurea specifically, in 438 women. 'Twice as likely' and 'other people with diabetes' are both looser and larger than what was published.
- 'METFORMIN MADE MONKEY BRAINS SIX YEARS YOUNGER' — decomposed: a methylation-clock delta in the frontal lobe of SIX male monkeys, scored against clocks the same laboratory trained on 36 monkeys from the same colony, in a study whose authors state in their own limitations section that it 'omitted assessments of monkey mortality.' A study that never measured survival was reported globally as evidence a drug slows aging.
- KONOPKA'S VO2MAX RESULT IS ROUTINELY CITED AS SIGNIFICANT. It was p = 0.08. The mitochondrial respiration (~25% training increase abolished) and insulin-sensitivity (30-50% attenuation) results are the significant ones. Cite those, not VO2max.
- MASTERS TABLE 2 RELATIVE-STRENGTH VALUES WERE WRONG IN THE ORIGINAL PUBLICATION and were replaced by corrigendum (corrected: placebo +18.9% vs metformin +14.8%, p=0.30). Anyone quoting the original strength row is quoting retracted numbers. The lean-mass and thigh-mass results were unaffected.
- THE TIER SLIP IN ONE SENTENCE: MILES (n=14, 6 weeks, messenger RNA from biopsies) and the monkey study (n=6 treated, clock endpoints, mortality not measured) are tier-1/tier-2 surrogate results. They are routinely cited in longevity media as tier-3 evidence that metformin slows human aging. Meanwhile the actual tier-3 evidence — DPPOS at 21 years — is null on both mortality (HR 0.99) and MACE (HR 1.03), and is almost never mentioned.
- THE DISSOCIATION THAT SETTLES IT: in the same DPP/DPPOS cohort over 21 years, metformin demonstrably prevented diabetes (-17% incidence) and demonstrably moved nothing downstream (all-cause mortality HR 0.99, cancer 1.04, CVD 1.08, MACE 1.03). Any article on metformin-for-longevity that omits this pairing is misleading by omission.
- FUNDING ASYMMETRY WORTH STATING PLAINLY: the positive founding study was funded by AstraZeneca/Bristol-Myers Squibb with a BMS employee as co-author and a pharma-funded consultancy supplying five authors. Both failed replications (Velux Foundation; and the Welsh study) and the null RCT (NIDDK/NIH, VA) were publicly or foundation funded with no declared industry interest. The direction of effect tracks the direction of the money.
- NUMBERS I COULD NOT REACH IN PRIMARY TEXT AND WHICH MUST BE RE-VERIFIED BEFORE PUBLICATION: the DPPOS mortality hazard ratios (ADA site returned 403; taken from search-reported abstract), the Simpson 2006 BMJ adherence odds ratios (taken from search-reported abstract), Suissa & Azoulay's 13/9/3 bias breakdown, and all Stevenson-Hoare 2023 point estimates at 3/5/20 years (paywalled — only direction and reversal point are sourced).

**证据缺口(搜过但不存在):**
- NO TARGET TRIAL EMULATION, 2024-2026, OF THE ACTUAL BANNISTER COMPARISON. I searched specifically for a modern TTE of metformin initiators versus matched non-diabetics for all-cause mortality. It does not exist. Every TTE I found uses an active diabetes comparator (Shadyab 2025: sulfonylurea) or asks a different question (Scottish CKD continuation study). The specific claim that launched metformin-as-geroprotector has been tested by exactly two conventional cohort studies (Denmark 2022, Wales 2023, both negative) and by zero modern causal-inference analyses.
- TAME HAS NO CLINICALTRIALS.GOV REGISTRATION. Verified by three independent API queries on 2026-07-28. There is no NCT number, no locked protocol paper with a prespecified statistical analysis plan that I could locate. For a trial with an eleven-year public profile, a named FDA-negotiated composite endpoint, and continuous media coverage, the absence of a registry entry is itself the most informative available fact — and it establishes that zero patients have been enrolled.
- NO PRIMARY AFAR DOCUMENT STATING TAME'S BUDGET OR FUNDS RAISED TO DATE. Every figure in circulation ($30-50M from STAT 2022, $50-75M elsewhere, ~$8M/year from Barzilai) traces to journalism or interview, never to a grant document, audited statement, or AFAR financial disclosure. The AFAR TAME page itself gives no number.
- NO RCT OF METFORMIN IN HEALTHY NON-DIABETIC ADULTS WITH A HARD CLINICAL OUTCOME HAS EVER REPORTED. VA-IMPACT is the first and does not read out until September 2029, and even its population is prediabetes WITH established atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease — not healthy ageing. Thirty years into mass use of this drug, the outcome question has never been randomised in the population the longevity claim is about.
- NO PUBLISHED ITP RE-ANALYSIS SHOWING METFORMIN BENEFIT IN THE SECOND HALF OF LIFE, despite Barzilai citing one as existing in his August 2025 interview ('there's a new study from the ITP, which re-analyzes their own data and shows that metformin wasn't good in the first half of the animals' lives but performed significantly better in the second half of life'). I could not locate it. Either unpublished, in press, or mischaracterised — flagged for re-checking.
- NO FEMALE PRIMATE DATA AT ALL. The only primate metformin study is male-only with six treated animals, and its authors say so in the limitations. There is no monkey replication, no dog data, no second-species primate confirmation.
- NO HEAD-TO-HEAD TRIAL OF METFORMIN VERSUS EXERCISE, and no trial of metformin-plus-exercise versus exercise alone with a hard clinical outcome — despite two randomised trials (Konopka, MASTERS) showing metformin degrades the physiological response to both aerobic and resistance training, and despite exercise having far stronger human outcome evidence than metformin. Given that the overlap population (older adults trying to stay healthy) is the same, this is the most consequential missing trial on this line.
- NO RETRACTION, ERRATUM, OR EXPRESSION OF CONCERN ON BANNISTER 2014. It stands uncorrected twelve years on and continues to be cited as the foundation of the metformin-longevity hypothesis, including by content that reaches consumers. The correction to the scientific record exists only as separate papers by other groups in other journals — a structural failure of the correction mechanism, not merely of one study.
- NO EFFECT SIZES AVAILABLE FOR THE WELSH 20-YEAR REVERSAL at the 3-, 5-, and 20-year timepoints. The BMC Public Health full text is paywalled; only the direction and the crossover point are sourced from the abstract. Someone with institutional access should extract the hazard ratios, because this is the paper that explains why Bannister's truncation mattered.
- COULD NOT REACH THE PRIMARY TAME DESIGN PAPER (Barzilai et al., Cell Metabolism 2016) — Cell.com and ScienceDirect both returned 403. The composite endpoint components and the FDA rationale in my claims come from AFAR's page and Barzilai's own 2025 interview, not from the design paper. Worth a second attempt with institutional access.
- UNVERIFIED AND EXCLUDED FROM CLAIMS: assertions circulating on AI-generated sites that ARPA-H made a major longevity investment in February 2026, that a '$38 million VITAL-H trial' of rapamycin/dapagliflozin/semaglutide is US-government funded, and that TAME enrolment is 'ongoing' as of early 2026. Only Barzilai's own August 2025 remark that TAME 'is now being handled within ARPA-H' is sourced. My web-search budget was exhausted before I could check VITAL-H against ClinicalTrials.gov — flagged for the fact-check pass, since a funded competitor trial would be a meaningful contrast to TAME's non-existence.

**来源清单:**
