### L7-16 **[承重]**

Loyal (Cellular Longevity, Inc.), LOY-001: on 28 November 2023 FDA's Center for Veterinary Medicine accepted the technical effectiveness portion of the conditional approval application for large-dog lifespan extension. The submission was 2,300+ pages comprising interventional studies plus an observational study of 451 dogs. Mechanism: reducing IGF-1 toward levels seen in smaller breeds. This was an acceptance of one technical section, not an approval; safety and manufacturing sections remained, and a confirmatory pivotal lifespan study must run in parallel after conditional approval.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:Loyal (Cellular Longevity, Inc.), 'FDA Center for Veterinary Medicine agrees Loyal's data supports reasonable expectation of effectiveness for large dog lifespan extension', 28 November 2023 (also Businesswire 20231127326868)
- URL:https://loyal.com/posts/loyal-announces-historic-fda-milestone-for-large-dog-lifespan-extension-drug
- 逐字引:> FDA CVM accepted "the technical effectiveness portion of our conditional approval application for LOY-001's use in large dog lifespan extension." Loyal's framing: "From our data, the FDA believes LOY-001 is likely to be effective for large dog lifespan extension."
- 利益相关:Company self-announcement; Loyal's fundraising depends on the milestone being read as significant.

### L7-17 **[承重]**

Loyal, LOY-002 (senior dogs, daily pill, caloric-restriction-mimetic framing): FDA CVM accepted 'reasonable expectation of effectiveness' (RXE) on 26 February 2025. Loyal's own post states this is 'NOT approval'. On 13 January 2026 FDA CVM accepted the Target Animal Safety (TAS) technical section of the Expanded Conditional Approval (XCA) application — two of three major technical sections now accepted, with Chemistry, Manufacturing and Controls (CMC) outstanding. The TAS package drew on multiple studies with over 400 dogs receiving LOY-002 for varying durations including up to one year, tested at 1x, 3x and 5x the intended clinical dose with no clinically significant adverse events observed. This is the exact milestone press coverage repeatedly renders as FDA 'approval', 'clearance' or 'backing'.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:Loyal, 'Not just for big dogs! Our senior dog program receives preliminary FDA efficacy acceptance (RXE)', 26 February 2025; Loyal, 'One step closer to a dog longevity drug: What LOY-002's safety milestone means to a practicing vet', 13 January 2026 (also Businesswire 20260113476778)
- URL:https://loyal.com/posts/loy-002-tas
- 逐字引:> Feb 2025 post: the company explicitly states this is "NOT approval." Jan 2026 post: FDA CVM accepted "the Target Animal Safety (TAS) section of our Expanded Conditional Approval (XCA) application for LOY-002"; safety testing used "standard target dose multiples (1x, 3x, and 5x the intended clinical dose), with no clinically significant adverse events observed at these doses"; "Until the drug is approved by the agency*, we are limited in how much detail we can provide about the testing process." Loyal's consumer site carries the repeated footnote "*FDA approval not guaranteed."
- 利益相关:Company self-announcement. Loyal has raised more than $150 million; each FDA milestone is a fundraising event.

### L7-18 **[承重]**

Loyal's own description of the pivotal STAY trial changed between statements: 1,000 dogs aged 10 years and older weighing at least 14 pounds (company post, 26 February 2025) versus 1,300 dogs, placebo-controlled, across the US, fully enrolled (company post, 13 January 2026). Loyal anticipated in February 2025 having 'completed all the regulatory requirements by the end of 2025'; as of January 2026 the CMC section was still outstanding and trade press reported Loyal expects to complete its final technical submission and apply for Expanded Conditional Approval the following year. No peer-reviewed publication of the LOY-001 or LOY-002 efficacy package was located.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:Loyal company posts, 26 February 2025 and 13 January 2026; Longevity.Technology, 'FDA clears safety step for Loyal's senior dog longevity drug', 14 January 2026
- URL:https://longevity.technology/news/fda-clears-safety-step-for-loyals-senior-dog-longevity-drug/
- 逐字引:> Longevity.Technology, 14 Jan 2026: FDA "acceptance" is not the same as approval; it means "the agency has reviewed Loyal's safety data and agrees that, under proposed conditions, the drug appears safe for use." If approved, LOY-002 "would become the first FDA-approved drug intended to extend lifespan itself, in any species." Loyal "has raised more than $150 million to date."
- 利益相关:Longevity.Technology is a longevity-industry trade publisher. Notably it is nonetheless one of the few outlets to state plainly that acceptance is not approval.

### L7-19 **[承重]**

There is no census of off-label rapamycin prescribing for aging. The only cited dataset is a self-selected, unblinded survey: Kaeberlein et al., 'Evaluation of off-label rapamycin use to promote healthspan in 333 adults', GeroScience (2023). N = 333 adults with a history of off-label rapamycin use plus 172 never-users. Outcomes were entirely self-reported; ratios greater than 3:1 in agreement were observed for self-reported improvements in health, happiness, brain function, feelings of youthfulness, confidence, calmness, anxiety and generalized aches and pains. The authors state the cohort was not blinded and may have been affected by placebo. A commercial page claims 'more than 24 medical practices currently prescribe rapamycin as an anti-aging treatment' — no verifiable source for that figure was found.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Kaeberlein M et al. Evaluation of off-label rapamycin use to promote healthspan in 333 adults. GeroScience. 2023. doi:10.1007/s11357-023-00818-1
- URL:https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s11357-023-00818-1
- 逐字引:> Survey data from 333 adults with a history of off-label use of rapamycin, and 172 adults who had never used rapamycin. "Ratios of greater than 3:1 in agreement were observed for self-reported improvements in health, happiness, brain function, feelings of youthfulness, confidence, calmness, anxiety, and generalized aches and pains." The cohort "was not blinded to their intervention and may have been impacted by some degree of placebo effect."
- 利益相关:Kaeberlein is a prominent public advocate for rapamycin research and has taken rapamycin himself; he later founded Optispan, a healthspan company. Respondents were recruited from rapamycin-user communities — maximally self-selected.

### L7-20 **[承重]**

Telehealth longevity vendors sell prescription rapamycin at consumer price points with lifespan-extension copy and a supplement-style disclaimer. AgelessRx's rapamycin page (fetched 28 July 2026) lists rapamycin 'Starting at $65' per month with 'free shipping | free medical visit', requires patient 'willingness to take off-label Rapamycin' plus mandatory bloodwork, and carries the DSHEA disclaimer on a page selling a prescription drug — a category error, since that disclaimer is a dietary-supplement construct. The marketing copy makes a species-spanning claim that is not accurate as written: rapamycin has not extended lifespan in every organism tested, and there is no human lifespan data at all.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:AgelessRx rapamycin product page, agelessrx.com/rapamycin/, fetched 28 July 2026
- URL:https://agelessrx.com/rapamycin/
- 逐字引:> "Proven to extend the lifespan of every organism tested so far"; "Shown to improve healthspan and quality of life in aging adults"; "Starting at $65"; "The above statements have not been evaluated by the Food and Drug Administration. This product is not intended to diagnose, treat, cure, or prevent any disease."
- 利益相关:Vendor selling the product. AgelessRx also runs its own trials (e.g. PEARL), so is simultaneously evidence-producer and seller.

### L7-21 

Longevity clinic price points and claim style, from vendor pages. Fountain Life (fetched 28 July 2026) markets 'Live longer, live healthier', 'Reprogram your aging' and an 'Advanced Longevity & Healthspan Membership', advertising that members can 'reverse accelerated brain aging' (46%) and achieve 'significant plaque reversal' (12%) — bare percentages with no N, no denominator, no comparator and no time window on the page. It offers whole-body MRI, '150 GB of data points' and AI-guided diagnostics; publishes no membership pricing; and carries a single medical disclaimer, which concerns stem cells. Biograph, co-founded by Peter Attia, is reported at $7,500/year for core membership (one-time deep assessment) and $15,000/year for 'Black' membership (ongoing specialist consults, at-home monitoring) — figures from secondary reporting, vendor page not fetched.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:fountainlife.com homepage, fetched 28 July 2026; Biograph pricing via secondary reporting (nmn.com / longevity.technology)
- URL:https://fountainlife.com/
- 逐字引:> "Live longer, live healthier"; "Reprogram your aging"; "Advanced Longevity & Healthspan Membership"; claims of "reverse accelerated brain aging" (46%) and "significant plaque reversal" (12%). Sole disclaimer: "Since Stem Cell Therapy is not FDA approved, you are encouraged to consult with your primary care provider before undergoing any stem cell therapy."
- 利益相关:Vendor page. Fountain Life is associated with Peter Diamandis, a longevity-industry promoter and author. Biograph figures are secondhand and should be re-verified.

### L7-22 **[承重]**

Bryan Johnson said publicly in a WIRED interview published 21 July 2025 that he was close to shutting down or selling Blueprint, his supplement company, and gave as his stated reason that the commercial business costs him credibility for his 'Don't Die' philosophy — not that the products don't work. He disputed New York Times reporting (March 2025) that Blueprint was falling short of its monthly break-even target by over $1 million, saying the company operates at break-even. On 24 July 2025 Fortune reported he was hiring a CEO for Blueprint. As of 28 July 2026 — one year later — I found no confirmation that Blueprint has actually been sold or shut down.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:WIRED profile by Katie Drummond, published 21 July 2025, reproduced on Blueprint's own site; Fortune, 24 July 2025; New York Times, March 2025 (not directly retrieved)
- URL:https://blueprint.bryanjohnson.com/blogs/news/wired-profile-bryan-johnson
- 逐字引:> "I am so close to either shutting it down or selling it"; "I don't want it"; "I don't need the money, and it's a pain-in-the-ass company"; "people see the business and give me less credibility on the philosophy side. I will not make that trade-off. It is not worth it to me."
- 利益相关:The copy of the WIRED profile fetched is hosted on Johnson's own commercial site — he controls the reproduction. The NYT reporting he disputes was not directly retrieved in this session.

### L7-23 **[承重]**

Blueprint's actual on-label claims are scrupulously DSHEA-compliant structure/function statements with no disease claims and no lifespan claim: 'ENERGY • FOCUS • RECOVERY', 'BRAIN • HEART • METABOLISM', 'VISION • VITALITY • CELL DEFENSE', 'GUT BARRIER • MICROBIOME • DIGESTION', plus the standard FDA disclaimer. Prices (fetched 28 July 2026): Essential Capsules $49, Longevity Mix (Blood Orange) $49, Advanced Antioxidants $49, Essential Microbiome $39, Omega-3 $39, Extra Virgin Olive Oil $39. This is the central commercial-layer mechanism worth naming: the label carries no efficacy claim, while the founder's publicly broadcast personal 'speed of aging' results carry the efficacy message that the label legally cannot.

- 来源类型:vendor-selfreport
- 出处:blueprint.bryanjohnson.com homepage, fetched 28 July 2026
- URL:https://blueprint.bryanjohnson.com/
- 逐字引:> Product claims listed: "ENERGY • FOCUS • RECOVERY", "ENERGY • IMMUNITY • COGNITION", "BRAIN • HEART • METABOLISM", "BRAIN • HEART • JOINT HEALTH", "GUT BARRIER • MICROBIOME • DIGESTION", "VISION • VITALITY • CELL DEFENSE". Prices: Essential Microbiome $39.00; Longevity Mix - Blood Orange $49.00; Essential Capsules $49.00; Extra Virgin Olive Oil $39.00; Omega-3 $39.00; Advanced Antioxidants $49.00. Disclaimer: "These statements have not been evaluated by the Food and Drug Administration. This product is not intended to diagnose, treat, cure, or prevent any disease."
- 利益相关:Vendor page.

### L7-24 **[承重]**

David Sinclair resigned as president of the Academy for Health and Lifespan Research (a body of roughly 60 aging scientists) in March 2024, after a February 2024 press release from Animal Bioscience — a company he founded and whose CEO is his brother Nick Sinclair — quoted him describing the dog supplement 'Leap Years' as 'the first supplement proven to reverse aging in dogs'. Matt Kaeberlein publicly called the statement 'a lie' and left the Academy; other members resigned in protest. Nir Barzilai, the incoming president, said: 'The data is not good, you're calling it the wrong thing, and then you're selling it.' The press release was subsequently corrected to remove 'proven to reverse aging in dogs'. Sinclair said the release contained an inaccurate quote and expressed regret.

- 来源类型:media-secondhand
- 出处:MIT Technology Review, 9 June 2026; nmn.com news, 'Dr. David Sinclair Steps Down from Prominent Aging Research Position'; Animal Bioscience press release, Businesswire 20240228209735, 28-29 February 2024
- URL:https://www.technologyreview.com/2026/06/09/1138545/david-sinclair-plans-to-test-whole-body-rejuvenation-drugs-in-the-xprize-competition/
- 逐字引:> MIT Technology Review, 9 June 2026: "In 2024, he resigned as president of the Academy for Health and Lifespan Research after claiming that a supplement developed by a company his brother runs had 'reversed' the age of dogs" — "a claim for which there was so little evidence that one scientist called it a 'lie.'" And: Sinclair "has frequently been criticized by other scientists for making unproven rejuvenation claims." Original press release quote as reported: "I am very proud of the teams at NCSU and Animal Biosciences, who, after years of collaborative research and a clinical trial, have developed the first supplement proven to reverse aging in dogs." Nir Barzilai: "The data is not good, you're calling it the wrong thing, and then you're selling it."
- 利益相关:Sinclair's commercial ties include Animal Bioscience (brother is CEO), Metro International Biotech (SAB head; the MIB-626 IND that triggered the NMN preclusion), Life Biosciences, Tally Health, InsideTracker, plus books. nmn.com is an NMN-industry-adjacent outlet. The Businesswire release itself timed out on two fetch attempts — the current corrected wording is unverified.

### L7-25 **[承重]**

The evidence underlying the 'reverse aging in dogs' claim was a subjective, short-window surrogate that the objective test did not corroborate. In the peer-reviewed version of the Leap Years study, 'improved cognitive function' was qualified as 'owner-assessed' and the abstract described effects as 'more subtle'. The primary endpoint reported a significant difference in CCDR (Canine Cognitive Dysfunction Rating) score across treatment groups from baseline to the primary endpoint at p=0.02, but the published version added that no difference was detected between groups using in-house cognitive testing. This is the cleanest single instance in the commercial layer of a 3-month owner-questionnaire score in dogs being marketed as proof of aging reversal.

- 来源类型:practitioner-blog
- 出处:SkeptVet, 'Evidence Update: Leap Years Supplement', June 2024, analysing the peer-reviewed publication versus the earlier preprint and press release
- URL:https://skeptvet.com/2024/06/evidence-update-leap-years-supplement/
- 逐字引:> "There was a significant difference in CCDR score across treatment groups from baseline to the primary endpoint (p=0.02)" — but the published version added that "No difference was detected between groups using in house cognitive testing." The preprint claimed "Improved Cognitive Function"; the peer-reviewed version added the qualifier "owner-assessed." SkeptVet: the company and Dr. Sinclair "promoted it heavily as a major advance" despite evidence being "underwhelming and more negative than positive overall."
- 利益相关:SkeptVet is an anonymous-authored veterinary skeptic blog — a practitioner blog, not peer review. The primary paper itself should be retrieved and the p-value, N and endpoint definitions re-verified before publication.

### L7-26 

Concern about the biomarker layer that longevity clinics sell is documented but predates the current boom. Dyke et al., 'Researcher perspectives on ethics considerations in epigenetics: an international survey', Clinical Epigenetics (2022): online survey of 189 epigenetics researchers working in 31 countries, fielded January-March 2020; 48.1% of respondents reported being 'very' or 'extremely' concerned about the growing availability of direct-to-consumer epigenetic tests. Caliber: researcher opinion, not a validation study, and collected roughly five years before the current epigenetic-clock retail market.

- 来源类型:peer-reviewed
- 出处:Dyke SOM et al. Researcher perspectives on ethics considerations in epigenetics: an international survey. Clinical Epigenetics. 2022. PMID 36056446; PMC9440515
- URL:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9440515/
- 逐字引:> From January to March 2020, researchers conducted an online survey of 189 epigenetic researchers working in 31 countries, assessing concern regarding four non-medical applications of epigenetics including direct-to-consumer testing; 48.1% of respondents reported being "very" or "extremely" concerned about direct-to-consumer epigenetic tests.

**交叉口径问题:**
- 'FDA acceptance of one technical section' is routinely rendered in press as FDA approval/clearance/backing. Loyal's LOY-002 has three technical sections in its Expanded Conditional Approval application (Reasonable Expectation of Effectiveness, Target Animal Safety, and Chemistry-Manufacturing-Controls); two are accepted, CMC is outstanding, and the pivotal STAY lifespan study must still complete for FULL approval. Loyal's own posts say plainly 'NOT approval' and footnote '*FDA approval not guaranteed' — the overstatement is in the press layer, not the company's formal language.
- NAD+ market figures mix incompatible scopes and are quoted interchangeably: bulk NAD ingredient (food + pharmaceutical grade) vs finished NAD-precursor consumer supplements vs a broader 'longevity market'. Circulating figures for essentially the same market: USD 488.17M (2021, GMI), USD 795.9M (2025), USD 876.2M (2025, FMI), USD 1.42B, USD 3.9B (2025, market.us). That is roughly a 5x spread on the same year. CAGRs range 6.1% to 18.2% for overlapping windows. Any single figure quoted in an article is a choice among these, and every one of them is marketing for a $2,450–$4,490 report.
- Longevity-clinic counts: a market-report assertion of 'more than 700 dedicated longevity clinics globally' sits alongside the only actual field survey, which covered 82 self-selected clinics. Neither is a census, and the 82-clinic survey's percentages (e.g. 'over one third offer stem cells') are frequently repeated as if they characterise the whole industry.
- Loyal's pivotal STAY trial N changed in the company's own statements: 1,000 dogs aged >=10 years weighing >=14 lb (26 Feb 2025) versus 1,300 dogs (13 Jan 2026). Same trial, same company, no explanation of the expansion given in either post.
- 'The first supplement proven to reverse aging in dogs' (Animal Bioscience, Feb 2024). The actual numerator is a change in owner-completed CCDR questionnaire scores at 3 months, p=0.02, in dogs — and the peer-reviewed version added that no difference was detected between groups on in-house objective cognitive testing, and re-labelled the cognitive result 'owner-assessed'. A subjective 3-month surrogate in a non-human species was promoted to a proven aging-reversal outcome claim.
- AgelessRx's rapamycin page: 'Proven to extend the lifespan of every organism tested so far.' Rapamycin lifespan extension is not universal across tested organisms, and there is no human lifespan data at all. The sentence silently spans species and evidence tiers while sitting above a $65/month checkout. The page also carries the DSHEA supplement disclaimer on a prescription-drug product — a category error indicating the copy was written for supplement compliance rules that do not apply.
- Fountain Life advertises '46%' (reverse accelerated brain aging) and '12%' (significant plaque reversal) as bare percentages with no N, no denominator, no comparator arm and no time window anywhere on the page.
- The NARB Tru Niagen decision (21 May 2026) states the general form of the entire cross-caliber problem in this field: 'the required substantiation depends on the message conveyed to consumers, not the advertiser's regulatory classification', and claims that 'went beyond cell-level mechanistic effects to promise perceptible or functional health benefits' were not supported. Raising NAD+ (a biomarker) does not license claims about heart, brain, immune or anti-aging benefits (outcomes). Verified biomarker movement being sold as felt benefit is the mechanism, and a self-regulatory body has now named it.
- Blueprint illustrates the label/brand split: the on-pack claims are strictly compliant structure/function statements with the FDA disclaimer and no lifespan claim, while the founder's publicly broadcast personal 'speed of aging' figures carry the efficacy message the label cannot legally make. Any critique that only audits label copy will find nothing wrong.

**证据缺口(搜过但不存在):**
- No verbatim text of the FDA 29 September 2025 NMN citizen-petition response letters was obtained. Both regulations.gov (document FDA-2023-P-0872-2754 and its attachment PDF) and every www.fda.gov URL returned 403/404 from this environment. All FDA language on NMN in this report is second-hand from law firms, trade associations and trade press and MUST be verified against the letters before publication.
- No FDA warning letter was found that targets an NMN, NAD+, or longevity-supplement company for AGING or LONGEVITY claims specifically. Every anti-aging-related warning letter located was triggered by DISEASE claims (cancer, stroke, Alzheimer's, hypertension, cataracts) or by labeling/manufacturing violations. This absence is itself the finding: under DSHEA, aging is not a disease, so 'supports healthy aging' is legally safe space and FDA enforcement never reaches it.
- No FTC enforcement action against a NAD+/NMN/longevity supplement marketer in 2024-2026 was found. The most recent US government consumer-protection actions on anti-aging claims are 2018 (TA Sciences / TA-65) and 2020 (ReJuvenation). Current-era pressure on longevity claims comes from industry self-regulation (NAD/NARB, driven by a competitor challenge) and private class actions, not from government.
- No peer-reviewed, governmental, or claims-database census of how many US clinicians prescribe rapamycin off-label for aging exists. The only quantitative dataset is a self-selected, unblinded, self-reported survey of 333 users recruited from rapamycin communities. A '24+ medical practices' figure circulates on commercial sites with no traceable source.
- No formal position statement on longevity clinics from any major professional society (AMA, American Geriatrics Society, Gerontological Society of America, AFAR, ACP) was located. The only published critique found is a single-author editorial in Aging (Albany NY), which contains no data.
- No independent, non-vendor estimate of the NAD+/longevity supplement market exists. Every figure in circulation traces to a market-research firm selling a report priced $2,450-$4,490, with unauditable methodology and no published sales panel. There is no government (Census, BLS, NHANES-linked) or academic estimate.
- Loyal has not published the LOY-001 or LOY-002 efficacy packages in a peer-reviewed journal. The 2,300-page effectiveness submission underlying the November 2023 milestone is not public, and the company states it is 'limited in how much detail we can provide' until approval. The most-cited FDA milestone in the longevity field rests on data no external scientist has read.
- One year after saying in July 2025 that he was 'so close to either shutting it down or selling it', no confirmation was found that Bryan Johnson has actually sold or shut down Blueprint. The company continues to sell product as of 28 July 2026.
- The class action against Niagen Bioscience / ChromaDex over Tru Niagen claims could not be pinned to a court, case number or filing date; only plaintiff-side coverage was reachable.
- Whether the Animal Bioscience February 2024 press release now carries a visible correction notice could not be verified — the Businesswire release timed out on two fetch attempts. The correction is reported second-hand only.
- Peter Attia's AMA #35 ('Anti-Aging Drugs — NAD+, metformin, & rapamycin', 16 May 2022, with Matt Kaeberlein) is behind a paid membership wall; the show-notes preview ends before the substantive discussion. His exact stated positions on NAD+ precursors could not be verified from primary source, only from secondary characterisations.
- Biograph membership pricing ($7,500 / $15,000 per year) is from secondary reporting only; the vendor page was not fetched. Fountain Life publishes no pricing at all, so the clinic sector's actual price distribution is not documentable from public sources.
- 21 CFR 101.93 could not be fetched (eCFR redirected to unblock.federalregister.gov). The DSHEA structure/function framework as stated here is from working knowledge plus the disclaimer text observed on a live vendor page; the statutory text should be checked against eCFR.
